The Duchess as an Auction Lot","volume":"婚后外传：不该出售的左脚凉鞋","anchor":"chapter-0130","paragraphs":["The auction catalogue offered a palace, a broken robot and a duchess's left sandal. Mara objected to the last entry. She had left the shoe by the nursery door while helping a child retrieve a kite; its appearance in a sale photograph suggested alarming speed. Cassian read the description aloud. \"Slightly worn. Considerable character.\"","\"If you bid, we are discussing your judgement.\"","He bought her lunch instead.","The estate had once been called a palace by its owner, who had three bedrooms and a magnificent talent for exaggeration. Anybody could see the joke from the modest gate. Its old mill now housed a community workshop. A nursery occupied one wing, while a reading group met in the living-room. Repair costs had become unaffordable. The committee had let bills accumulate until the whole project stood on a razor edge. Brigid, the caretaker, had agreed to sell unwanted furniture to fund the work, but the overall catalogue included property merely lent by residents. Their quilt had been mistaken for hers; a borrowed rug was being advertised as a rare import.","Kit, a young restorer, waited in the lobby with her partner Dara. The high-profile event had required makeup and a formal jacket that did not feel like hers. She was talented with tile and terrified of the saleroom adviser. He had told her not to overstep by questioning ownership. Now she was stressed enough to twist a handkerchief into knots. Mara offered her the catalogue and a marker. \"Show me what you know.\"","Not only the furniture but also a child's artwork had been priced. A paper miniature of the house was labelled antique. Kit pointed to a photo of the maker, still only nine. The virtual preview had used the wrong folder. Beyond the roundabout, a local media outlet had promoted it already, which made admitting the error unpleasant. It would not make continuing honest. Its launch should be conditional on checking the loans, even if that seemed bureaucratic or tiresome. They needed to restructure the sale before bidders could show up tomorrow.","Dara led Mara through the rotunda. A pendulum swung above a mosaic floor that Kit was repairing one segment at a time. A layer of grit threatened the fresh setting. A delivery lorry had cut across the roadside verge, scattering mud through the entrance. Its driver was here for a pickup, not entitled to take anything with a price sticker. Cassian asked him to wait while Brigid checked the list.","In the workshop, an elderly clock repairer adjusted a regulator. He had borrowed a laser measuring tool from a lab to check the new bench layout. Kit used a lever to free a sticking cupboard, then found scissors where nobody had expected them. \"People put things down,\" she said. \"Then the catalogue makes their confusion look authoritative.\"","Eli, the new caretaker, was being blamed for a missing purse. He had once used heroin, and the adviser treated that history as an answer to every question. Eli was in recovery and had worked here for months without complaint. His past did not establish what had happened to Brigid's bag. Mara asked where it was last seen. \"Near the oven,\" Brigid said. \"I was checking the ingredient list for lunch.\"","The adviser had not bothered to ask.","A small jet crossed the sky beyond the orchard. Inside the nursery, a purifier hummed by the window. A damaged light bulb had left the corridor dim, so Eli had moved the children to the lounge. He was trying to replace it when the accusation began. A pill packet in his pocket was his own labelled prescription. Mara did not need its contents announced to a room of strangers.","Cassian found Brigid's purse inside a sack of donated costumes. The lining had caught on a jacket. A jean patch was pinned beside a skirt and a strip of theatrical lace. Somebody had swept the bag into the sack while tidying. Brigid apologised to Eli immediately. The adviser said the matter was settled. Eli said it was not settled for him.","Kit's laptop sat beside the costume pile. Her keyboard was clogged with dust, but the original inventory remained readable. One item was an old rifle, disabled and locked in a display case. It was not a plaything to sell without proper checks. Another was an oar donated by a retired railroad worker; his family had retained ownership. Kit's carefully written notes had vanished from the public file.","\"A non-standard import,\" the adviser said, blaming the software. The engineer could help put together a test. The system could effectively misfile a note, but a person still had to check the result. Scientifically testing a program did not mean believing every screen. Dara called the engineer who had configured it. She agreed to cooperate and compare the files in a low stress condition, without the adviser hovering over her shoulder.","Meanwhile, a reading-group packet lay on the table. It included a text about the American defense contractor Science Applications International Corp. The adviser had mistakenly assigned that packet a price too. Its members were studying attribution, not selling research to fund the roof. Mara could understand a careless import; she could not understand defending each exposed mistake as necessary policy.","They crossed the hedge to the pub on the high street to call the owner. A pub notice offered financial advice from a visiting volunteer, with no claim that a poster could solve every debt. The elected residents' chair was already there. Brigid asked, straight up, whether a loan note could attest to ownership. The chair said it could help establish what had been agreed. The infrastructure needed quite a lot of work, he acknowledged. But repair bills could not pollute an ownership record or turn a loan into a gift.","Dara's worn sandal broke on the way back. Kit knelt to fix its strap with a tiny piece of cord. Their brief, fierce smile made Mara turn tactfully toward Cassian. He was looking at her as though he had caught her being happy. Then a crash came from the loading yard, and everyone ran."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1020,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0130\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：不该出售的左脚凉鞋</div><h1>1. The Draft That Became a Price","volume":"婚后外传：不该出售的左脚凉鞋","anchor":"chapter-0131","paragraphs":["A crate had fallen from the lorry, spilling the broken robot onto the gravel. Its arm landed beside a pole holding the visitor sign. It lay upon a folded sack, its plastic casing split. The driver had loaded it because the adviser said the inventory was approved. Kit had not approved it. Neither had its maker, who was now sitting in the nursery wondering why someone had sold his school project.","Mara climbed onto the low loading step and checked the remaining labels. Not only the robot but also the quilt from the reading room was aboard. Kit's repaired mosaic panel had been wrapped for transport too. The overall error was bigger than a lost annotation. The auction adviser had selected a later file in which every uncertain item was marked for sale. His explanation did not penetrate the room. Its title said DRAFT. He had removed that word from the public catalogue.","\"You can't overstep into ownership merely because a sticker prints,\" Mara said. \"Unload the disputed items.\"","He warned that a cancellation would make the entire event unaffordable. Brigid looked stressed, but Eli brought her the original loan ledger. The old palace could restructure the sale around objects she actually owned. Its mill and nursery needed help; that did not justify taking a neighbour's property. Quite a lot of work remained, and they could begin without pretending it was already complete.","Dara steadied a ramp while Cassian carried the mosaic panel down. She checked the quilt lining for damage too. Kit checked every segment for a new crack. One corner had lost a layer of packing, but the tiles were intact. Her relief emerged as a laugh so sudden she covered it with a handkerchief. Dara kissed her dusty forehead. \"Your face is also a little non-standard.\"","\"My face is not on sale.\"","\"Excellent. I couldn't afford the reserve.\"","Inside the rotunda, the engineer opened the virtual preview on Kit's laptop. She asked permission before touching the keyboard. A comparison showed that the software had imported both the ownership note and the original maker's name. The adviser had hidden those columns to simplify the view. The engineer could attest to that sequence from the saved files. She would not claim to know his intentions. Scientifically, the records supported a narrower conclusion, which was enough to demand an explanation.","At the saleroom office, the adviser tried to rifle through Kit's private notebook. Cassian moved it out of reach. The locked historical rifle downstairs remained under Brigid's control; it would not be part of the revised event. The loaned oar went back to the railroad worker's daughter. A pub volunteer carried it across the lawn like a victorious rower until a branch caught the blade. Kit helped free it before its owner could object.","The nursery reopened after Eli replaced the light bulb. A child reached for the oven knob in the shared kitchen; Brigid gently moved him away and closed the gate. She checked the biscuit ingredient list again because one family needed a clear answer. The purifier had been donated with instructions, and she followed them rather than add a scented bottle to it. Cleaning products must not pollute the air people were trying to keep comfortable.","Eli set his own pill packet in a private locker. The adviser muttered another reference to heroin. Brigid stopped him. Eli's treatment history was not merchandise, and it was not evidence of today's missing purse. She had already apologised. The adviser was now being deliberately cruel to someone whose work had kept the building usable. The elected chair asked him to leave the public rooms while the contract was reviewed.","In the lounge, Dara found the catalogue photograph of Mara's sandal. The left shoe lay beside a miniature chair, making both look the same size. A paper marker had hidden the scale. That was almost funny. The missing ownership notes were not. Dara saved a clean photo before the listing disappeared, then called the media outlet that had promoted the event. It agreed to publish the correction beside its original report.","The children needed a quieter hour, so Kit moved their craft class to the living-room. They cut paper with rounded scissors and made a replacement window shutter for a model house. The rotary cutter stayed with an adult. A paperback beneath the table held a child's drawing of a rocket. Cassian read it aloud with such solemn emphasis that Mara had to leave the room to laugh.","Outside, a shower swept through the orchard. Eli brought in the costumes before they could soak. The skirt and jean patch remained together, but he separated the sharp pins. Brigid found the missing shampoo in a visitor's bag and left it at reception beside a note about the lavatory. A cracked outlet near the sink had been isolated for a qualified repair. It was small, ordinary infrastructure, yet neglecting it could make a whole wing unpleasant or unsafe.","Mara examined a harness hung by the potting shed. It belonged to a child's pony, not an antique dealer. The owner would show up later to collect it. A loose hedge branch had scratched the leather. She photographed that condition too, preventing a new argument about when it happened. The clock repairer meanwhile fixed the regulator above the door. Its pendulum marked the newly calm afternoon.","The adviser returned carrying a reading-group packet about the American defense contractor Science Applications International Corp. He suggested auctioning its handwritten annotations as famous corporate material. No member had such an association. The group had merely discussed a text. They meant to infuse reading with curiosity, not inflate an auction price. Brigid asked him, straight up, whether he intended to sell anything he could place on a table.","He had no useful answer. Sorting the files was tiresome, but letting errors accumulate would be worse. Mara watched the retreating lorry, now carrying only its driver's own empty crates. They still had to launch a truthful sale tomorrow. This time, she thought, they knew what they had."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1007,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0131\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：不该出售的左脚凉鞋</div><h1>2. What the House Could Keep","volume":"婚后外传：不该出售的左脚凉鞋","anchor":"chapter-0132","paragraphs":["The revised sale began with a new rule: the volunteer at the door would return lost footwear before offering anyone a catalogue. Mara received her left sandal on a velvet cushion. Cassian applauded. She wore a green skirt and an expression that warned him the cushion would be used against him if the ceremony continued.","Kit had removed the makeup she disliked. She had slept little, but she was less stressed. Dara brought her tea and a clean shirt, then stood on tiptoe to kiss her before opening the doors. They had been together for two years; today Kit introduced her as her partner without lowering her voice. The small confidence had nothing to do with the auction. It made the room brighter anyway.","Brigid had decided to restructure the event rather than cancel it. Not only the staff but also the visitors offered practical help. Donated skills would raise funds alongside unwanted furniture. Kit offered a future restoration workshop. Eli offered a guided history walk during his paid hours. The building's repairs remained unaffordable from ticket income alone, so the committee would seek additional grants. Quite a lot could change without claiming that one cheerful day had solved the accounts.","At the high street entrance, a volunteer met an elected councillor who had come to lobby for a safe pedestrian crossing. The roundabout made the short journey from the bus stop needlessly difficult. A roadside jet of muddy water from a passing van had soaked yesterday's visitors. The councillor promised to inspect the drainage request, not to obtain instant funding by smiling for a camera. Infrastructure could be improved without another heroic rescuer arriving at the last moment.","They could launch the smaller sale honestly. The sale opened in the saleroom beneath a repaired ceiling. Bidders could see the live room or join the virtual preview, which now showed ownership and condition notes. Brigid had expected only a few bidders to show up; the room filled. The first item was a chest Brigid wanted to part with. The second was a brass door knob. The old rug and the family quilt stayed in the reading-room; their owners had agreed to longer loans. A pub collection had paid for fresh storage boxes, a useful gift without a speech attached.","Kit put together a display of workshop tools to show how repair happened. The laser measuring instrument went back to the lab after its loan ended. A hand drill remained beside the rotary attachment, clearly labelled for a demonstration. A repaired harness was collected by its actual owner, who gave the nursery pony a fond pat. Nobody had to argue that living things were merchandise because they appeared in a catalogue image.","The house clock kept time above the door. Cassian used a lever to shift the remaining bench with Eli's help, checking that the route stayed clear. The old mill display was now secure. Eli explained how silt could penetrate a poorly fitted cover. Its small wheel turned in a shallow channel, demonstrating motion without blocking the lavatory path. A visitor who had complained of bureaucratic fuss yesterday thanked them for making the building easier to use. Kit accepted the thanks without pretending the earlier remark had pleased her.","Mara found the paperback under a chair in the lounge. She returned it to the child who had drawn the model shutter, then helped him hang a paper flag from the little pole. His robot would be repaired in the next workshop rather than displayed as a tragic casualty. Dara could modify the damaged joint, and the maker would help. A mistake had cost him time, but adults were finally listening when he explained his own machine.","The reading group met for a short session before lunch. Its packet again included a text about the American defense contractor Science Applications International Corp. This time the notes explained their sources, and no sale label obscured the title. They discussed how a shortened description could change a reader's inference. The assignment was challenging in a low stress condition; public humiliation had never improved it. The group planned to infuse the next event with more conversation and less solemn introduction.","Eli took the morning's photographs to the kitchen. A local journalist wanted to use one with his name attached, and he agreed. He did not agree to a story that reduced him to his past addiction. Brigid supported him. The journalist asked a different question about the repair work. By noon, the man who had been treated as guilty yesterday was discussing how the old oven could be replaced when funds allowed.","The adviser had been paid only for completed work under the contract. The committee would review the disputed charge and ask him to repay any sum found unjustified. Brigid did not ask Mara to pronounce a verdict. She asked her to read the corrected inventory one last time. Mara checked it upon the workbench, then signed as a witness to the items she had actually examined. She would not overstep to make the page look more impressive.","The shower had passed. Dara hung a newly washed curtain in the doorway and called Kit outside. Her wet hair smelled of shampoo. The chapel roof across the orchard no longer looked like it might fall into the picture. The two women sat on the grass, sharing a biscuit while children ran past. Kit had needed neither a high-profile scandal nor special financial advice to begin speaking. She had needed someone to cooperate when she said the inventory was wrong.","Mara and Cassian left through the garden gate. A lorry waited for the legitimate pickup, loaded with the sold chest and two worn tables. The public rooms had kept their furniture, and the nursery could remain open. The future was still on a razor edge: the roof needed money, and nobody would take a donation for granted. Yet selling things they did not own would only pollute that future with new harm.","Cassian offered Mara his arm. \"I hope you enjoyed your brief career as an auction lot.\"","\"Unforgettable.\"","\"I was prepared to bid quite high.\"","\"You already won me,\" she said, and pulled him into the shade for an appropriately private celebration."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1038,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0132\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：不该出售的左脚凉鞋</div><h1>3. A Live Player","volume":"婚后外传：屋顶舞会的真实独奏","anchor":"chapter-0133","paragraphs":["Mara's invitation to the rooftop ball arrived with a warning: costumes compulsory, weapons theatrical, donations optional. Cassian read the second clause twice before selecting a velvet coat and a blunt stage sword. His wife wore a silver textile gown repaired by Kit, with a wool wrap for the river wind. When she emerged from the bedroom, he forgot the complaint he had been rehearsing about his boots.","\"Will I do?\" she asked.","\"I intend to spend the evening providing evidence.\"","The ball occupied an old department store between two skyscraper towers. Its roof garden faced the river, while its basement housed a youth music programme. A violin concert would raise money for new instruments. Mara had come to restore a tile panel beside the balcony, not perform a theft. She had nevertheless noticed three possible exits before accepting a drink at the bar. Some habits were durable. Cassian asked her to hold onto his arm while a porter moved equipment across the doorway.","Lena, the young soloist, waited behind a curtain with a veil pinned around her shoulders. She was lucky to inhabit a city with a free youth programme, she said. She was radiant until a coordinator mentioned the corporate board paying for the stage. Then she became a visibly stressed-out woman clutching an expensive violin she did not own. The coordinator said she must not embarrass her sponsor. Lena said nothing. Mara preferred an overt problem to that strained silence.","In the early days of the music programme, Lena had borrowed her instrument from the canteen after washing dishes. Her teacher now hoped to obtain a permanent loan. The shrewd coordinator, Vesta, had attached the sponsor's logo to every photograph. She described the evening as business-friendly, though no ordinary music shop could afford its advertising fee. A scornful remark about \"community standards\" made Lena flush.","Cassian asked a technician to acquaint him with the stage route. A stereo test was playing through a small transistor amplifier beside a model spacecraft from last year's science performance. The model's antenna had been bent in storage. Its presence was harmless; the power cable beneath it was not. A boot could catch the cord. Mara moved the cable away from the walking line and asked the technician to secure it properly.","\"Sound faults can result from very ordinary damage,\" he said. \"We shouldn't let the programme run on auto-pilot.\"","A rehearsal began. Lena raised her bow, and the recording played a fraction too soon. Her accompanist stopped at once. Vesta blamed nerves. The technician looked puzzled. The stereo track had not been intended to play during the live solo. A screen was showing a duplicate schedule from a different event. The error was noteworthy because someone had manually selected it five minutes before the rehearsal.","Lena went to the bathroom. Mara followed only after hearing a crash. A soap dish lay broken in the basin; the young woman had knocked it down while reaching for a tissue. She sat beside a single slipper from a costume change, trying not to cry. \"If I complain, she says I'm ungrateful.\"","Mara handed her a clean towel. \"You can be grateful and still have ears.\"","Backstage, the wardrobe closet was crowded with clothing. A small doll had fallen from a child's bag, its hair tangled around a comb. Kit patiently freed it while Dara found a cushion for Lena. Kit helped tidy the abandoned costume rack. Neither pretended a compliment was a cure-all. They made space for her to breathe. Kit then examined the finely stitched gown and removed a loose hook that had been scratching her wrist.","Outside, workers were opening a trench near a broken streetlight. A worker in a helmet checked the lifting gear. A spade leaned against the fence; a wagon waited for displaced stone. The marked route remained clear, but a new delivery had blocked it. Cassian asked the driver to shift the load. The upward view from the roof was spectacular; the useful exit was at ground level. Safety rules should apply to a charity event too.","Mara returned to the tile panel. A swab and mild cleaning method suited its surface; a harsh product could destroy the remaining colour. She worked slowly while Kit checked the textile curtain. Kit examined the weave before choosing a matching thread. A little decoration concealed a torn seam. The wall thermometer showed a comfortable temperature, though wind made the stage draughty. Mara kept her wool wrap within reach.","Near the fireplace display, a wreath commemorated the music teacher who founded the programme. The inscription mentioned a local fishery that had paid the first year's rent. Its workers still sent contributions per household. A sponsor could help without pretending nobody else had ever mattered. The corporate board needed not just a flattering speech but an honest account of what its money supported.","Vesta returned from the airport with a photographer, who had been waiting for another guest. She proposed that Lena merely pose while the backing track played. It would be virtually impossible to catch in the final video, she said. Mara put down the swab and took a moment to ponder her reply. Cassian watched the pause with deep interest.","\"I'm unafraid of a live performance,\" Lena said before Mara could speak. \"I'm afraid you will make it impossible, then call me incompetent.\"","That was a noteworthy distinction. Vesta called it embarrassing. The technician quietly saved the current screen settings. Mara looked at Cassian, who was already holding the exit door open for the arriving teacher. The evening might still become a ball. First, someone needed to stop conducting it as an advertisement."],"questions":[],"wordCount":941,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0133\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：屋顶舞会的真实独奏</div><h1>1.
